H. Engine Cowl TAI Valve Installation

SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-001 (1) Remove the protective covers from the ducts and valve [5]. SUBTASK 30-21-11-210-001 (2) Examine the seal [2] as follows: (a) Make sure that the seal [2] do not have cracks, dents, or other damage. (b) Replace all damaged seal [2]. SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-002 (3) Do these steps to install the valve [5]: CAUTION DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH FORCE TO THE AFT DUCT . DAMAGE TO THE AFT DUCT CAN OCCUR. (a) If removed, install a temporary support between the aft duct and fan case bracket to move the aft duct rearward. NOTE: T emporary support can be installed between the fan case bracket and aft duct to hold the duct in this position. This will make the installation of the valve easier. (b) Install a seal [2] in the forward end of the valve [5] and on the aft duct flange. (c) Install the valve [5] between the ducts. 1) Make sure that the flow arrow points forward and locking feature on the downstream flange is correctly engaged. (d) Remove the temporary support between the aft duct and fan case bracket. (e) Loosely install the coupling [1] to attach the valve [5] to the ducts. 1) Make sure that the coupling nuts on the couplings are on top and the nuts face outboard. CAUTION MAKE SURE THAT THE COUPLINGS DO NOT APPLY A FORCE ON THE DUCTS OR THE INLET-COWL TAI VALVE. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , DAMAGE TO THE EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (f) Do these steps to tighten the coupling [1]: 1) If it is difficult to attach the valve [5] to the adjacent ducts, remove the lockwire from the link [4] and adjust the links until no force is applied to the ducts or the valve [5]. 2) Tighten the link [4] and install a MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048. 3) Use a torque wrench, STD-1014, to tighten the coupling [1] to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m) - 85 in-lb (9.6 N·m). 4) Lightly hit the surface of each coupling [1] with a rubber mallet, STD-3906. 5) Use a torque wrench, STD-1014, to tighten the coupling [1] again to 70 in-lb (7.9 N·m) - 85 in-lb (9.6 N·m). 6) Install the bolts [11] and nuts [12] that attach the aft duct to the fan case bracket. 30-21-11 a) Tighten the bolts [11] to 97.0 in-lb (11.0 N·m) - 103.0 in-lb (11.6 N·m). <1> Use a torque wrench, STD-1014 to tighten the bolts [11]. SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-005 (4) Connect the electrical connector DP1303 [10] to the valve [5]. (a) Make sure that there is clearance between the TAI valve and adjacent structures. SUBTASK 30-21-11-760-002 (5) If was removed, install the bonding jumper [8] to the flange. (a) Clean the bonding surface on the flange with alcohol, B00130. (b) Attach the bonding jumper [8] to the flange with the bolt [13], washers [14], and nut [15]. (c) Use an non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614, to measure the resistance between the bonding jumper [8] and aircraft structure. RYR 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-377, 386-416; AIRPLANES WITH TAI VALVE P/N 3215618-4 AND ON 1) Make sure that the resistance is 0.001 ohm (1.0 milliohm) or less. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 378-385, 417-999; AIRPLANES WITH TAI VALVE P/N 3215618-2/-3 2) Make sure that the resistance is 0.008 ohms (8.0 milliohms) or less. RYR ALL (d) Apply the adhesive, A50102, around the connection to encapsulate the bolt [13], washers [14], and nut [15]. SUBTASK 30-21-11-760-001 (6) Do these steps to connect the bonding jumper [8] to the valve [5]: (a) Clean the bonding surface on the valve [5] with alcohol, B00130. (b) Attach the bonding jumper [8] to the valve [5] with the bolt [6], washer [7], and nut [9]. NOTE: To minimize slack between the two attached lugs of the bonding jumper, rotate the lug at the valve forward and/or the lug on the fan case toward the upward 12 o'clock position of the fan case. Do not allow any riding condition (slack) of the jumper on the valve or the engine fan case. (c) Use an non-intrinsically safe bonding meter, COM-614, to measure the resistance between the bonding jumper [8] and valve [5]. 1) Make sure that the resistance is 0.008 ohms (8.0 milliohms) or less.

I. Engine Cowl TAI Valve Installation Test

SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-009 (1) For the valve on the No. 1 power plant, to this step: (a) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name A 6 C00148 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 & WING CONT A 7 C01001 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 1 COWL AI VALVE SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-024 (2) For the valve on the No. 2 power plant, to this step: 30-21-11 (a) Close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 6 C00149 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENGINE 2 CONTROL B 7 C01002 ANTI-ICE & RAIN ENG 2 COWL AI VALVE SUBTASK 30-21-11-910-001 CAUTION DO NOT KEEP THE COWL TAI VALVE OPEN FOR MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED TIME WHEN THE TEMPERATURE IS MORE THAN THE SPECIFIED LIMIT . DAMAGE TO THE INLET COWL CAN OCCUR. (3) If the outside air temperature is more than 50°F (10°C), operate the engine cowl anti-icing system for 30 seconds or less. (4) There is no time limit for operation of the engine cowl anti-icing system when the temperature is at or less than 50°F (10°C). SUBTASK 30-21-11-840-001 (5) Do one of the following steps to provide pneumatic pressure for the ducts: (a) To use the engines to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Start the Engine Procedure (Selection), TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00. (b) To use the Auxiliary Power Unit (APU) to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to the Pneumatic System with the APU, TASK 36-00-00-860-803. (c) To use an external air supply to pressurize the TAI duct, do this task: Supply Pressure to the Pneumatic System with an External Ground Air Source, TASK 36-00-00-860-802. (d) If the APU, external air, or a single engine is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then do these steps: 1) Put the BLEED 1 and 2 switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF position. 2) Put the L PACK and R PACK switches on the air conditioning panel in the OFF position. 3) Make sure that the ISOLATION VALVE switch is in the OPEN or AUTO position. 4) If the APU or external air is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then manually open the PRSOV valves. 5) If the a single engine is used to pressurize the TAI duct, then manually open the PRSOV valve on the non-running engine. 6) Open a PRSOV valve, do these steps on the applicable engine or engines: WARNING DO THESE SPECIFIED TASKS IN THE CORRECT SEQUENCE BEFORE YOU OPEN THE THRUST REVERSER: RETRACT THE LEADING EDGE, DO THE DEACTIVATION PROCEDURES FOR THE LEADING EDGE AND THE THRUST REVERSER (FOR GROUND MAINTENANCE), AND OPEN THE FAN COWL PANELS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE ABOVE SEQUENCE, INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. a) Open the left thrust reverser, do this task: Open the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-801-F00. 30-21-11 b) Put the applicable engine BLEED switch, on the P5-10 forward overhead panel, to the ON position. WARNING PRESSURIZE THE BLEED AIR SYSTEM BEFORE YOU OPEN THE PRSOV. WHEN YOU OPEN THE PRSOV, USE A RACHET TYPE WRENCH. THE PRSOV CAN OPEN QUICKLY AND THIS CAN CAUSE THE HANDLE OF THE WRENCH TO TURN. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . c) Use a wrench on the manual override nut for the PRSOV to put it to the OPEN position. d) Make sure that the PRSOV stays in the OPEN position. NOTE: The PRSOV is spring-loaded to the closed position. If there is pressure supplied and the PRSOV does not stay open, there may be a leakage in the PRSOV or in the sense line(s). WARNING OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE PROCEDURE TO CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS. IF YOU DO NOT OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS WHEN YOU CLOSE THE THRUST REVERSERS, INJURIES TO PERSONS OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. e) Close the thrust reverser, do this task: Close the Thrust Reverser (Selection), TASK 78-31-00-010-804-F00. SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-010 (6) Do this test of the COWL VALVE OPEN and COWL ANTI-ICE light switches: (a) Push and release the COWL VALVE OPEN and COWL ANTI-ICE light switches on the P5-11 panel. (b) Make sure that each light comes on then goes off. SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-011 (7) Make sure that the engine and wing anti-ice panel commands the applicable cowl TAI valves open: (a) For the left cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the ON position. 1) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 6 seconds. 2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light is on dimly after 6 seconds. 3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the Common Display System (CDS) display, above the left N1 speed display. (b) For the right cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the ON position. 1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 6 seconds. 2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light is on dimly after 6 seconds. 3) Make sure that the green TAI indication shows on the CDS display, above the right N1 speed display. 30-21-11 SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-015 WARNING DO NOT TOUCH THE COWL TAI DUCTS OR TAI VALVE WHEN THEY ARE HOT . IF YOU ARE NOT CAREFUL, INJURIES TO PERSONS CAN OCCUR. (8) Make sure that there is no leakage around the engine cowl TAI ducts and TAI valve. (a) To repair jet blast leakage, align the couplings. SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-012 (9) Make sure that the engine and wing anti-ice panel commands the cowl TAI valves closed: (a) For the left cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 1 switch in the OFF position. 1) Make sure that the left COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 3 seconds. 2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off after 3 seconds. (b) For the right cowl TAI valve, put the ENG ANTI-ICE 2 switch in the OFF position. 1) Make sure that the right COWL VALVE OPEN light comes on brightly for 1 to 3 seconds. 2) Make sure that the COWL VALVE OPEN light goes off after 3 seconds.

SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-014 (2) If the APU or external air is used to pressurize the TAI ducts, then, do this task: Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System, TASK 36-00-00-860-806. Task Description

2. Honest Orifice - Cleaning (Figure 701)

A. General

(1) This procedure provides the steps necessary to remove, clean, and install the honest orifice on those engine cowl Thermal Anti-Ice (TAI) valves that have a removable honest orifice.

B. References

Reference Title 36-00-00-860-806 Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System (P/B 201) 71-11-02-010-801-F00 Open the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201) 71-11-02-410-801-F00 Close the Fan Cowl Panels (P/B 201)

D. Consumable Materials

E. Location Zones

Zone Area 410 Subzone - Engine 1 420 Subzone - Engine 2 F. Prepare for the Cleaning SUBTASK 30-21-11-870-001 (1) If not already done, do this task: Remove Pressure from the Pneumatic System, TASK 36-00-00-860-806. SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-018 (2) For the right fan cowl panel, do this task: Open the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-010-801-F00. 30-21-11

G. Honest Orifice Cleaning

SUBTASK 30-21-11-020-006 (1) Do the following to remove the honest orifice [4]: (a) Remove the lockwire [1]. (b) Remove the screw [2] and washer [3]. (c) Remove the honest orifice [4]. SUBTASK 30-21-11-170-001 (2) Clean the orifice with one of the following methods: NOTE: You can replace the honest orifice with a new or serviceable part as an alternative to cleaning it. (a) Method one uses compressed air: 1) Apply an air source, STD-6638, through the orifice from the threaded end and at each of the four vent holes. 2) If necessary, use a 0.032 in. (0.813 mm) lockwire to clean out the internal hole of the honest orifice. (b) Method two uses ultrasonic cleaning: 1) Put the orifice in a solution of Bonderite C-AK MXP Aero cleaner, B50270, for 4 to 6 minutes. 2) Flush the orifice internally and externally with clean water at high pressure. 3) Put the removable orifice in a beaker with the open end of the orifice down and fill the beaker with Bonderite C-AK MXP Aero cleaner, B50270. 4) Put the beaker in an ultrasonic cleaner, STD-182, and fill the tank with water until the water level is 0.375 in. (9.525 mm) from the top of the beaker. 5) Operate the ultrasonic cleaner for 5 minutes. 6) Remove the orifice from the beaker and discard the dirty cleaning solution. 7) Flush the orifice and beaker with the cleaning solution using a squeeze bottle, STD-6462. 8) Put the removable orifice back in the beaker with the open end of the orifice down and fill the beaker with the cleaning solution. 9) Operate the ultrasonic cleaner for another 5 minutes. 10) Repeat the process from step 6 until the cleaning solution is clear after a 5 minute period of operation in the ultrasonic cleaner. SUBTASK 30-21-11-100-001 (3) Use swab, G01659, to carefully clean and wipe up dirt from the following: NOTE: Be careful not to allow dirt to drop down into the hole. (a) The valve housing. (b) The honest orifice threads. SUBTASK 30-21-11-100-002 (4) If unwanted materials fall into the valve port, use the vacuum cleaner, STD-183, to remove them. 30-21-11 SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-003 CAUTION OBEY THESE INSTRUCTIONS. IF YOU INSTALL THE HONEST ORIFICE INCORRECTLY , HOT AIR LEAKS WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (5) Use thread lubricating compound for the assembly of all threaded parts as follows: CAUTION USE OF TOO MUCH THREAD LUBRICATING COMPOUND CAN CAUSE CLOGGED VENTS AND ORIFICES. (a) Apply a small quantity of Liqui-Moly NV Thread Compound, D50179, to the threads of all male threaded parts. SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-006 (6) Install the honest orifice [4]. (a) Tighten the honest orifice [4] to 17 in-lb (1.9 N·m) - 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m). (b) Make sure that there is a minimum of 0.015 in. (0.381 mm) gap under the honest orifice head for sufficient airflow (View A-A, Figure 701). SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-007 (7) Install the washer [3] and screw [2]. (a) Tighten the screw [2] to 6 in-lb (0.7 N·m) - 8 in-lb (0.9 N·m). SUBTASK 30-21-11-160-002 (8) Remove all of the thread lubricating compound that is remaining after assembly of the threaded parts. SUBTASK 30-21-11-420-008 (9) Use the lockwire [1] (MS20995C20 lockwire, G50225) to attach both the screw [2] and honest orifice [4] as one to the housing.

H. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 30-21-11-860-020 (1) For the right fan cowl panel, do this task: Close the Fan Cowl Panels, TASK 71-11-02-410-801-F00.

E. AUTO Air Data Sensor Heating Operational Test

SUBTASK 30-31-00-750-003 WARNING KEEP PERSONNEL, AND EQUIPMENT AWAY FROM THE PITOT PROBES, TOTAL AIR TEMPERATURE PROBE, AND ANGLE-OF-ATTACK VANE. THESE COMPONENTS WILL BECOME VERY HOT . THEY CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONNEL, AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT . (1) Do this check of the following lights on the window and pitot heat module: (a) Make sure the CAPT PITOT , L ELEV PITOT , L ALPHA VANE, TEMP PROBE, F/O PITOT , R ELEV PITOT , R ALPHA VANE, and AUX PITOT lights on the window and pitot heat module go on. (2) Test the AUTO position of the PROBE HEAT A and B switches. NOTE: Only one engine should be operating while performing this test of the AUTO position of the PROBE HEAT switches. This is done to test the full health of the system. (a) Do this task for engine 1: Start the Engine Procedure (Selection), TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00. (b) With the engine running, check the following lights on the window and pitot heat module: 1) Make sure the CAPT PITOT , L ELEV PITOT , L ALPHA VANE, TEMP PROBE, F/O PITOT , R ELEV PITOT , R ALPHA VANE, and AUX PITOT lights on the window and pitot heat module go off. (c) Do this task for engine 1: Stop the Engine Procedure (Usual Engine Stop), TASK 71-00-00-700-819-F00. 1) Make sure the CAPT PITOT , L ELEV PITOT , L ALPHA VANE, TEMP PROBE, F/O PITOT , R ELEV PITOT , R ALPHA VANE, and AUX PITOT lights on the window and pitot heat module go on. (d) Do this task for engine 2: Start the Engine Procedure (Selection), TASK 71-00-00-800-807-F00. (e) With the engine running, check the following lights on the window and pitot heat module: 1) Make sure the CAPT PITOT , L ELEV PITOT , L ALPHA VANE, TEMP PROBE, F/O PITOT , R ELEV PITOT , R ALPHA VANE, and AUX PITOT lights on the window and pitot heat module go off. (f) Do this task for engine 2: Stop the Engine Procedure (Usual Engine Stop), TASK 71-00-00-700-819-F00. 1) Make sure the CAPT PITOT , L ELEV PITOT , L ALPHA VANE, TEMP PROBE, F/O PITOT , R ELEV PITOT , R ALPHA VANE, and AUX PITOT lights on the window and pitot heat module go on. 30-31-00 RYR 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-168, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-999; RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 169 POST SB 737-30A1063 (Continued) F. AUTO Air Data Sensor Heating Operational Test - Alternative Procedure NOTE: The alternative method does not require engines running. D. Window Heat System Operational Test

SUBTASK 30-41-00-080-001 CAUTION ALL PROTECTIVE CAPS AND COVERS MUST BE REMOVED FROM ALL AIR DATA SENSORS. THE SENSORS AND COVERS COULD BE DAMAGED IF THERE ARE COVERS INSTALLED ON THE SENSORS WHEN THEY ARE HEATED. (1) Make sure that there are no protective covers on the air data sensors. (a) If protective covers are found, then remove them before you continue this test. 30-41-00 SUBTASK 30-41-00-840-001 (2) Use the Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or pyrometer, STD-1179, or other temperature indicating device near each window temperature sensor to measure the window temperature. SUBTASK 30-41-00-880-001 (3) If the window temperature is greater than 110°F (43°C), then cool the window. NOTE: The window can be cooled with cool water or by providing shade from the sun. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-001 (4) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT switches on the Window and Pitot Heat Module to the on position. (a) Make sure that the corresponding ON light on the window and pitot heat module comes on. NOTE: It can take up to 15 seconds for the light to come on. (b) Make sure that both master caution lights are off. SUBTASK 30-41-00-210-001 (5) Wait for the windows to heat up. (a) Make sure that the front and side windows stabilize at 90°F (32°C) to 120°F (49°C). NOTE: It can take up to 4 minutes for the window temperatures to stabilize. RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017 (b) Make sure that the number 4 and number 5 windows heat up to more than 90°F (32°C) but less than 125°F (52°C). RYR ALL SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-002 (6) Put the WINDOW HEAT TEST OVHT/PWR switch to the OVHT position for 1 second. (a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights come on. (b) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION lights come on. (c) Make sure that ANTI-ICE light comes on. (d) Make sure that the ON lights go off in less than 70 seconds. (e) Make sure that the windows start to cool. (f) Wait until the window temperatures go below 110°F (43°C). SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-003 (7) One switch at a time, put each WINDOW HEAT power switch in the off position for 1 second and then put the switch in the on position. (a) Make sure that the applicable OVERHEAT light goes off. (b) Make sure that the window heat ON light comes on. (c) Make sure that the windows begin to heat. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-004 (8) Press either MASTER CAUTION light. (a) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION light goes off. (b) Make sure that the ANTI-ICE light goes off. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-005 (9) Wait 4 minutes. 30-41-00 SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-006 (10) Hold the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST not more than 60 seconds. (a) Make sure that the WINDOW HEAT ON lights stay on for each window. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-011 (11) Release the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch. (a) If holding the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST causes the OVERHEAT light to illuminate, the WINDOW HEAT ON lights shall go off within 70 seconds. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-007 (12) Put the WINDOW HEAT power switches in the off position. SUBTASK 30-41-00-860-010 (13) If the PWR TEST switch caused an overheat condition, then press either MASTER CAUTION light. (a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights, the MASTER CAUTION lights, and the ANTI-ICE lights are off.

30-41-11-440-801Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Override Mode Activation/Deactivation ATA 30 · Task 440-801

Task Description

2. Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Override Mode Activation/Deactivation

A. General

(1) This task includes the steps to activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode on the Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU). (2) The WHCU 1 is on the E2-1 rack and supplies power for the No. 1 left and No. 2 right windows. (3) The WHCU 2 is on the E4-2 rack and supplies power for the No. 1 right and No. 2 left windows. (4) In the OVERRIDE mode, the OVERHEAT lights will come on when the two window heat sensors become defective.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

D. Prepare for the Activation/Deactivation

SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-036 (1) Make sure that these circuit breakers are closed: F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-11 Row Col Number Name B 8 C00393 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT SIDE B 9 C00228 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT FRONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-12 Row Col Number Name B 8 C00394 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT FRONT B 9 C00392 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT SIDE SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-025 (2) To get access to the electronic equipment center, do this step: 30-41-11 (a) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

E. OVERRIDE Mode Activation/Deactivation for No. 1 Window

SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-026 (1) Activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode for the No. 1 window: (a) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position. (b) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the ON position. (c) For the No. 1 left window: 1) Push and hold the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY buttons on the WHCU 1 for 5 seconds. (d) For the No. 1 right window: 1) Push and hold the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY buttons on the WHCU 2 for 5 seconds. (e) For activation, make sure that the #1 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light comes on. (f) For deactivation, make sure that the #1 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light goes off. (g) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position. RYR 397-999; Airplanes with the dual sensor operation (WHCU program pin P1-5 is grounded) F. OVERRIDE Mode Activation/Deactivation for No. 2 Window NOTE: The No. 2 window dual sensor configuration and OVERRIDE mode is available only for airplanes with the two WHCU configuration with part number 83000-2790X. To use dual sensor configuration on the No. 2 window, the WHCU program pin (P1-5) should be grounded and the two window sensors should be connected. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-031 (1) Activate/deactivate the OVERRIDE mode for the No. 2 window: (a) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position. (b) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the ON position. (c) For the No. 2 left window: 1) Push and hold the FAULT HISTORY and BIT LAMP RESET buttons on the WHCU 2 for 5 seconds. (d) For the No. 2 right window: 1) Push and hold the FAULT HISTORY and BIT LAMP RESET buttons on the WHCU 1 for 5 seconds. (e) For activation, make sure that the #2 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light comes on. (f) For deactivation, make sure that the #2 WINDOW green OVERRIDE MODE light goes off. (g) Set the applicable WINDOW HEAT switch, on the P5-9 panel, to the OFF position. RYR 397-999 30-41-11

G. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

30-41-11-000-803Heat the Window - Heat Gun ATA 30 · Task 000-803

H. Heat the Window - Heat Gun

SUBTASK 30-41-21-880-002 (1) Do these steps to heat the windows if the temperature is too cold: NOTE: These steps can be used if the ambient temperature is significantly less the specified test temperature range of 68°F (20°C) - 108° (42°C). (a) Heat the window near the sensor with a 180 deg. F (82 deg. C) maximum output temperature heat gun, STD-442, for up to thirty seconds. NOTE: You can use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 (including pyrometer, STD-1179), or other temperature indicating device to do a check of the temperature. SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-010 (2) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window. (a) Remove the WHCU, do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-803. 30-41-21 RYR 397-999 (Continued)

I. Resistance of the Window Temperature Sensors Measurement

SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-005 (1) Measure the resistance of the applicable window temperature sensor. (a) Apply pressure to the corner near the temperature sensor terminal when measuring the resistance of the sensors. NOTE: It is necessary to apply the pressure to the terminals to find an intermittent connection that can occur at any point along the temperature sensor. (b) Make sure that the resistance of the sensor is within the limits shown on Figure 505. Table 503/30-41-21-993-805 WINDOW TEMP SENSOR CONNECTOR/PINS WHCU WDM LEFT FRONT 1 (Primary) D16230/ 19 TO 25 M321 30-41-11 LEFT FRONT 1 (Spare) D16230/ 26 TO 27 M321 30-41-11 RIGHT SIDE 2 (Primary) D16230/ 17 TO 18 M321 30-41-11 RIGHT SIDE 2 (Spare) D16230/ 17 TO 18 M321 30–41–11 RIGHT FRONT 1 (Primary) D16232/ 19 TO 25 M323 30-41-12 RIGHT FRONT 1 (Spare) D16232/ 26 TO 27 M323 30-41-12 LEFT SIDE 2 (Primary) D16232/ 17 TO 18 M323 30-41-12 LEFT SIDE 2 (Spare) D16232/ 17 TO 18 M323 30–41–12 NOTE: Window temperatures of 68°F (20°C) - 108°F (42°C) are expected for this test. NOTE: If the temperature of any individual window in Table 503 is greater than 108°F (42°C) due to environmental condition(s), then a measured sensor resistance value less than 362 ohms is acceptable. NOTE: A measured sensor resistance value above 362 ohms indicates a failed sensor. SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-006 (2) Do this test to measure the resistance of the spare sensors for the side windows (Table 503): NOTE: This test applies to spare sensors without quick disconnect plugs. (a) Move the rubber terminal boot covers to expose electrical terminals and mounting screws. NOTE: The terminals are found at the bottom aft corner of the No. 2 Windows. (b) Examine the terminals for corrosion. 1) If it is necessary, clean the terminals. (c) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the primary sensor. (d) Install the D terminal lug to the spare sensor. (e) Make sure that the resistance is within the limits (Figure 505). (f) Remove the D terminal lug that is attached to the spare sensor. (g) Install the D terminal lug to the primary sensor. 30-41-21 RYR 397-999 (Continued) (h) Apply a layer of Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, to the terminals with the swab, G01659. NOTE: The Dow Corning 340 heat sink compound, D50244, is intended to prevent inadvertent loss of insulation between the sensor terminals and the metal frame of the window, that can cause the window to overheat. (i) Install the rubber terminal boot covers over the terminals as required. 1) Make sure that the rubber terminal boot covers are securely installed to avoid dislocation. SUBTASK 30-41-21-760-008 (3) Do this test to measure the resistance of the spare sensors for the side windows (Table 503): NOTE: This test applies to spare sensors with quick disconnect plugs. (a) Remove the quick disconnect plugs from the channel. (b) Disconnect the quick disconnect plug for the primary sensor. (c) If it is necessary, examine the quick disconnect plugs for the primary sensor and spare sensor for corrosion and clean. (d) Connect the quick disconnect plug for the spare sensor. (e) Make sure that the resistance is within the limits (Figure 505). (f) Disconnect the quick disconnect plug for the spare sensor and reconnect the quick disconnect plug to the primary sensor. (g) Place the quick disconnect plugs back in the channel.

J. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 30-41-21-420-003 (1) Install the WHCU (TASK 30-41-11-400-803).

(g) Push the LAMP TEST and BIT VERIFY switches simultaneously and hold for 4 seconds. 1) Make sure that the module is in the #1 WINDOW OVERRIDE mode. (h) If necessary, push and hold the LAMP TEST switch and BIT VERIFY switch again to turn off the #1 WINDOW OVERRIDE mode. 30-41-11-710-801Window Heat Control Unit System - Test ATA 30 · Task 710-801

Task Description

2. Window Heat Control Unit System - Test (Figure 501)

A. General

(1) The Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU)s are installed in the electrical/electronic (EE) compartment. A Built-In-Test Equipment (BITE) module is installed on all the control units. The test makes sure the WHCUs operate properly. (2) The WHCUs can be tested separately or they can all be tested at the same time.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 117 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Left 118 Electrical and Electronics Compartment - Right 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 117A Electronic Equipment Access Door

E. Test the Normal Functions of the WHCUs

SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-003 (1) Make sure the window temperature is less than 110°F (43°C). NOTE: If the window temperature is more than 110°F (43°C), WHCU will not supply electrical power to heat the window. (2) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT power switches on the window/pitot heat control panel, P5–9, to the ON position. NOTE: If you are only testing one WHCU, you only need to put the applicable switch in the ON position. (b) Make sure that both MASTER CAUTION lights on the glareshield panel, P7, are off. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-004 (3) Put the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/PWR TEST switch to the OVHT position for 1 second. (a) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights come on. (b) Make sure the MASTER CAUTION lights come on. (c) Make sure ANTI-ICE light comes on. (d) Make sure the ON lights go off in less than 70 seconds. (a) Make sure that the applicable OVERHEAT light goes off. (b) Make sure the window heat ON light comes on. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-006 (5) Press either MASTER CAUTION light. (a) Make sure that the MASTER CAUTION light goes off. (b) Make sure that the ANTI-ICE light goes off. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-007 (6) Wait 4 minutes. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-008 (7) Hold the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST not more than 60 seconds. (a) Make sure that the WINDOW HEAT ON lights stay on for each window. SUBTASK 30-41-11-860-019 (8) Release the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch. (a) If holding the WINDOW HEAT OVHT/ PWR TEST switch to PWR TEST causes the OVERHEAT light to illuminate, the window heat ON lights shall go off within 70 seconds. (a) Make sure that the green BIT TEST OK lights come on for 15 seconds. 30-41-11 SUBTASK 30-41-11-740-008 (4) Push the LAMP TEST switch. 30-41-21-000-801Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film ATA 30 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film (Figure 501)

A. General

(1) The number 1 and number 2 windows have a layer of material which heat the windows when electricity is applied. The Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) provides power to heat the windows. The WHCUs have various output connections which should be matched to each window resistance. This test checks the electrical resistance of the window heaters and makes sure the heaters are matched to the WHCU.

B. References

Reference Title 30-41-11-000-801 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal (P/B 401) 30-41-11-400-801 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Installation (P/B 401)

30-41-21-760-802Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film ATA 30 · Task 760-802

Task Description

4. Check the Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film

A. General

(1) The number 1 and number 2 windows have a layer of material which heat the windows when electricity is applied. The window heat control units (WHCUs) provide power to heat the windows. The WHCUs will measure the resistance of the window at start-up and then adjust the output voltage and power accordingly.

B. References

Reference Title 30-41-11-000-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal (P/B 401) 30-41-11-400-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Installation (P/B 401)

SUBTASK 30-41-21-010-004 (1) Remove the applicable WHCU for the window, do this task: Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal, TASK 30-41-11-000-803.

E. Electrical Resistance of the Window Heat Film Check

SUBTASK 30-41-21-970-002 (1) Measure the resistance at each connector to ground as follows. (a) Unit 1 Left Fwd M321 D16230 pin 1 to ground. (b) Unit 1 Right side M321 D16230 pin 3 to ground. (c) Unit 2 Right Fwd M323 D16232 pin 1 to ground. (d) Unit 2 Left side M323 D16232 pin 3 to ground. SUBTASK 30-41-21-970-003 (2) Make sure that the window resistance is within the range of 31.4 to 52 ohms for the front windows (No. 1), and 55.7 to 100 ohms for the side windows (No. 2). F. G. Heat the Window - WHCU

SUBTASK 30-41-21-880-003 (1) Do these steps to heat the windows if the temperature is too cold: NOTE: These steps can be used if the ambient temperature is significantly less the specified test temperature range of 68°F (20°C) to 108°F (42°C). (a) One switch at a time, put the WINDOW HEAT switches, on the window and pitot heat module, to the ON position. 1) Make sure that the corresponding ON light on the window and pitot heat module comes on. NOTE: It can take up to 15 seconds for the light to come on. 2) Make sure that both master caution lights are off. (b) Wait for the windows to heat up. 1) Use an Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 (including pyrometer, STD-1179), or other temperature indicating device near each window temperature sensor to measure the window temperature. 2) Make sure that the front and side windows stabilize at 90°F (32°C) - 120°F (49°C). NOTE: It can take up to 4 minutes for the window temperatures to stabilize. (c) Put the WINDOW HEAT power switches in the OFF position. 1) Make sure that the OVERHEAT lights, the MASTER CAUTION lights, and the ANTI-ICE lights are off. 30-41-21-700-801Measure the Insulation Resistance and Dielectric Absorption Ratio of the Control Cabin Windows ATA 30 · Task 700-801

Task Description

6. Measure the Insulation Resistance and Dielectric Absorption Ratio of the Control Cabin Windows (Figure 506)

A. General

(1) This Predictive Maintenance & Enhanced Troubleshooting (PMET) procedure is optional and the results are for evaluation purposes only. No action is required as a result of this inspection if a flight deck effect or other observed fault does not exist. The recommendations are made to show Boeing's best practices to make sure that maximum aircraft reliability is reached. If a flight deck effect or other observed fault exists, it must be corrected with accepted procedures. (2) This test measures the Insulation Resistance (IR) and Dielectric Absorption Rate (DAR) of the control cabin window heat sensors, conductive coating, and bus bars. A low dielectric insulation resistance value between the components in the window can cause the system to not operate correctly. A check of these values can help to monitor the health of the components in the window. (3) This test only does a check of the sensor on the L2/R2 window that is connected to the system. To do a check of the spare sensor on the L2/R2 window, refer to WDM 30-41-11 or WDM 30-41-12 to change the connection of the sensor to the circuit.

B. References

Reference Title 20-10-39-700-801 Insulation Resistance Testing - Special Detail Inspection (P/B 501) 24-22-00-860-811 Supply Electrical Power (P/B 201) 24-22-00-860-812 Remove Electrical Power (P/B 201) 25-11-21-000-801 Flight Compartment Forward Ceiling Panel Removal (P/B 201) 30-41-11-000-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Removal (P/B 401) 30-41-11-400-803 Window Heat Control Unit (WHCU) Installation (P/B 401) 30-41-11-710-801 Window Heat Control Unit System - Test (P/B 501) WDM 30-41-11 Wiring Diagram Manual WDM 30-41-12 Wiring Diagram Manual

C. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. G. Window Heat Insulation Resistance Check

SUBTASK 30-41-21-710-003 (1) Use the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to do the IR and DAR test of the control cabin L2 and R2 windows (TASK 20-10-39-700-801): NOTE: The Fluke multimeter will show an “ERR” message at the end of the test if the DAR value is more than 1.6. NOTE: The Megger multimeter will show 3 horizontal lines on the display at the end of the test if the DAR value is more than 1.6. (a) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor, and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows: 1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to the first test point on Table 504. 2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798 or equivalent meter to 50 Volts Direct Current (VDC). NOTE: This check makes sure that the circuits are isolated and there are no shorts. 3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum of 15 seconds. a) If the value shown on the meter is above 50 megohms, continue. b) If the value is below 50 megohms, do a check of the wiring between the applicable M2675 coil cord assembly and the window: <1> If the wiring is satisfactory, replace the applicable window. 4) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504. a) Do not record the values found. (b) Do an IR check of the circuit insulation between the conductive coating and the sensor, and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows: 1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to the first test point on Table 504. 2) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500 VDC. 3) Push the TEST button on the meter for a minimum 10 seconds. 4) Record the IR value found in Table 504. 5) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504. (c) Do a DAR check between the conductive coating and the sensor, and between the sensor and the Ground Bus Bar on the L2 and R2 windows: 1) Connect the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to the first test point on Table 504. 2) Select the DAR function on the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter. 3) Set the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter to 500 VDC. 4) Push the TEST button on the insulation resistance multimeter, COM-17798, or equivalent meter. 30-41-21 RYR 397-999 (Continued) 5) Record the DAR value found in Table 504. 6) Do the check again at each point shown in Table 504. 30-41-31-000-801Thermal Switch Removal ATA 30 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Thermal Switch Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) This task gives instructions to remove the Thermal Switch. (2) The window number 5 thermal switches are installed near the top of the window. (3) The windows have markings to indicate where to install the switch. A spring holds the thermal switch against the window.

B. Location Zones

Zone Area 212 Flight Compartment - Right

C. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 30-41-31-860-001 (1) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name D 1 C00226 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL RIGHT FRONT AC D 2 C00225 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT SIDE AC E 1 C00224 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT FRONT AC E 2 C00227 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL RIGHT SIDE AC F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-11 Row Col Number Name RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 7 C00229 WINDOW HEAT POWER L4 & L5 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 8 C00393 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT SIDE B 9 C00228 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT FRONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-12 Row Col Number Name RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 7 C00395 WINDOW HEAT POWER R4 & R5 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 8 C00394 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT FRONT B 9 C00392 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT SIDE SUBTASK 30-41-31-010-001 (2) Remove the trim from around the window. 30-41-31

D. Thermal Switch Removal

SUBTASK 30-41-31-020-001 (1) Remove the thermal switch [2]. (a) Disconnect the electrical leads [1] from the thermal switch [2]. (b) Pull the spring from the thermal switch [2]. (c) Remove the thermal switch [2] from the window. NOTE: Be careful not to damage the fairing compound. If the switch does not come off by hand, you may tap the switch side with a no bounce hammer. If you use a hammer to remove the switch, you will have to discard the switch.

30-41-31-400-801Thermal Switch Installation ATA 30 · Task 400-801

Task Description

3. Thermal Switch Installation (Figure 401)

A. Tools/Equipment

NOTE: When more than one tool part number is listed under the same "Reference" number, the tools shown are alternates to each other within the same airplane series. Tool part numbers that are replaced or non-procurable are preceded by "Opt:", which stands for Optional. Reference Description COM-1572 Thermometer - Infrared, Intrinsically Safe Part #: 568 EX Supplier: 89536 Part #: 568 EX/BR Supplier: 89536 Part #: 568 EX/CN Supplier: 89536 Part #: 568 EX/ETL Supplier: 89536 Opt Part #: DHS24XC-FM Supplier: 08086 Opt Part #: DHS24XF-FM Supplier: 08086 Opt Part #: EX-MP4 A Supplier: 3GT36 Opt Part #: IR-16L3 IS Supplier: 75037 STD-1179 Tester - Pyrometer

B. Consumable Materials

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Prepare to Install the Thermal Switch

SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-008 (1) Remove the old fairing compound from the window. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-009 (2) Clean the face of the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-010 (3) Clean the surface of the window where it will touch the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-011 (4) Prepare the fairing compound, A00214, or thermal heat sink compound, G50427, that bonds the thermal switch [2] to the window. NOTE: Optional fairing compounds include: Granger 610-1016 DC340 or adhesive, A50061. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-012 (5) Find the correct location to install the thermal switch [2]. NOTE: It could be necessary to change the position of the bracket. 30-41-31 (a) Look for the arrow or vertical line that is machined onto the metal insert, visible from the inboard surface of the window. 1) If the window does not have the arrow or vertical line, locate the label that reads "LOCATE SWITCH". (b) Put the vertical center-line of the thermal switch [2] as near as possible to the location indicated by the positioning arrow or vertical line. 1) If there is no arrow or vertical line, place center-line of thermal switch within 1 in. (25.4 mm) of label. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-013 NOTE: Omit this step when using thermal heat sink compound, G50427. (6) Put MS122 lubricant, D00095, on the surface of the thermal switch [2] that touches the window. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-014 (7) Put the excessive thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, on the face of the thermal switch [2] that touches the window. NOTE: Optional fairing compounds include GRANGER 610-1016 DC340 and adhesive, A50061. CAUTION DO NOT APPLY TOO MUCH COMPOUND ON THE SWITCH. TOO MUCH COMPOUND CAN CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE WINDOW. (a) Use only a very thin layer of thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, just enough to cover the surface of the switch when it is against the window with the retaining spring engaged.

E. Thermal Switch Installation

SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-003 (1) Put the thermal switch [2] in the spring bracket. (a) Pull the spring back. (b) Make sure the terminals on the thermal switch [2] are at the hinge of the spring. (c) Engage the spring with the slot on the rear of the thermal switch [2]. (d) Let the spring push the thermal switch [2] against the window. (e) Release the spring. (f) Remove all thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, that comes out against the window. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-015 NOTE: Omit this step and its substeps when using thermal heat sink compound, G50427. (2) Make sure the bond is satisfactory. NOTE: Permit one hour for the sealant to become hard. When the temperature is less than 50°F (10°C), more time is necessary. (a) Look through the window at the bond. (b) Make a new bond if you see the following: 1) Bubbles with a diameter of more than 3/32 in. (2.4 mm). 2) Bubbles that add to more than 10 percent of the bond. 30-41-31 SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-016 (3) Clean excess thermal heat sink compound, G50427, or compound, A00214, from the area of the bond and the thermal switch [2] with solvent, B00083. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-018 (4) If it is necessary, trim the liner and re-install the trim around the window. NOTE: It is acceptable to leave the notch from the previous switch as is. SUBTASK 30-41-31-420-004 (5) Connect the electrical leads [1] to the thermal switch [2]. F. Thermal Switch Installation Test SUBTASK 30-41-31-710-001 (1) Do a test of the thermal switch [2]. (a) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name D 1 C00226 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL RIGHT FRONT AC D 2 C00225 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT SIDE AC E 1 C00224 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL LEFT FRONT AC E 2 C00227 WINDOW HEAT CONTROL RIGHT SIDE AC F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-11 Row Col Number Name RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 7 C00229 WINDOW HEAT POWER L4 & L5 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 8 C00393 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT SIDE B 9 C00228 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT FRONT F/O Electrical System Panel, P6-12 Row Col Number Name RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 7 C00395 WINDOW HEAT POWER R4 & R5 RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-071 PRE SB 737-56-1017 B 8 C00394 WINDOW HEAT POWER RIGHT FRONT B 9 C00392 WINDOW HEAT POWER LEFT SIDE (b) Push the applicable window heat switch to the ON position. (c) Monitor the inner temperature of the window adjacent to the thermal switch [2] with pyrometer, STD-1179, or Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572. (d) If the temperature stays between 65°F (18.3°C) and 105°F (40.6°C) for window No. 3 and 80°F (27°C) and 125°F (52°C) for window No. 5, then you have corrected the fault. NOTE: It may take more than 15 minutes for the window to heat. SUBTASK 30-41-31-860-002 (2) Put the window heat switch in the OFF position.

SUBTASK 30-41-41-420-003 WARNING THERE ARE HIGH VOLTAGES IN THE P5 OVERHEAD PANEL. MAKE SURE YOU DO NOT TOUCH OR SHORT EXPOSED TERMINALS. INJURY TO PERSONNEL OR DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (3) Attach the connectors to the window/pitot heat module [1]. SUBTASK 30-42-00-710-001 (3) Do these steps to make sure that the windshield wiper system operates correctly: CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE WIPER ON A DRY OR DIRTY WINDSHIELD. ALWAYS SUPPLY WATER TO THE WINDSHIELD BEFORE YOU OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO THE WINDSHIELD. (a) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield. NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth. (b) Do these steps to test the intermittent speed operation of the wiper: 1) Set the windshield wiper switch to INT (P5 overhead panel flight compartment). NOTE: The test for the pilot's and first officer's wipers are the same. Use the applicable wiper switch to operate the wiper. 2) Make sure that the wiper blade parks near the lower edge of the window during the off time of the intermittent cycle. 3) Make sure that the wiper blades do not touch the metal structure. NOTE: Minimum overlap between the wiper blades and the windshield seal is permitted. 4) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the windshield. 30-42-00 5) Make sure that the wipers make two complete strokes every 6 to 8 seconds. NOTE: One stroke = one wipe. (c) Do these steps to test the low speed operation of the wiper: 1) Set the windshield wiper switch to LOW. NOTE: In the LOW position, the wiper blade moves at approximately 160 strokes per minute. 2) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the windshield. NOTE: One stroke = one wipe. (d) Do these steps to test the high speed operation of the wiper: 1) Set the windshield wiper switch to HIGH. NOTE: In the HIGH position, the wiper blade moves at approximately 250 strokes per minute. 2) Make sure that the operation of the wiper is smooth and it clears the water from the windshield. NOTE: One stroke = one wipe. (e) Set the windshield wiper switch to LOW. 1) Make sure that the wipers go to low speed operation. (f) Set the windshield wiper switch to INT . 1) Make sure that the wipers go to intermittent operation. (g) Set the windshield wiper switch to PARK. 1) Make sure that the wiper blades park near the lower edge of the glass. (h) Stop the water spray to the window.

G. Windshield Wiper Arm Installation

SUBTASK 30-42-31-020-009 (1) If necessary, loosen the adjustment screw [4]. SUBTASK 30-42-31-640-002 (2) Apply grease, D00013, to the serrations on the adjustment sleeve [10] and motor-converter shaft. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-007 (3) Install the arm assembly [3] on the motor-converter shaft. (a) Place the arm assembly [3] on the motor-converter shaft in the parked position. NOTE: To determine the position of the wiper arm assembly and for adjustment instructions refer to Figure 201. 1) For large adjustments, turn the arm assembly [3] and adjustment sleeve [10] together on the motor-converter shaft. 2) For small adjustments, turn the adjustment sleeve [10] within the arm assembly [3]. NOTE: For fine adjustments of the arm assembly, remove the adjustment sleeve from the arm and turn the sleeve in the same direction that the arm must be moved. Each notch moves the wiper end about 0.034 in. (0.864 mm). 30-42-31 (b) Apply 3.5 lb (1.6 kg) to 4.5 lb (2.0 kg) downward force at the blade attach point. 1) Make sure that the blade assembly [9] stays on the glass. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-008 (4) Install the bolt [5]. (a) Make sure that the head of the bolt [5] is on the same side as the lockwire hole in the wiper arm. (b) Tighten the bolt [5] to hold the arm assembly [3] and adjustment sleeve [10] in their positions. 1) Make sure that the arm assembly [3] and the adjustment sleeve [10] are above the end of the motor-converter shaft. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-009 (5) Install the washer [8] and bolt [7] on the end of the motor-converter shaft. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-013 (6) Tighten the bolt [7] to 20 in-lb (2.3 N·m) – 25 in-lb (2.8 N·m). SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-014 (7) Make sure that the adjustment sleeve [10] and arm assembly [3] touch the bolt [7] and washer [8] (View A-A, Figure 201). (a) Make sure that there is no clearance. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-012 (8) Tighten the bolt [5] to 45 in-lb (5.1 N·m) – 50 in-lb (5.6 N·m). SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-006 (9) If it is removed, do this task: Windshield Wiper Blade Installation, TASK 30-42-11-400-801. SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-011 (10) Do this task: Windshield Wiper Arm Force - Check/Adjustment, TASK 30-42-31-820-801. SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-012 (11) Do this task: Windshield Wiper Arm Position - Check/Adjustment, TASK 30-42-31-820-802. SUBTASK 30-42-31-420-011 (12) Install the lockwire [6] through the hole in the bolt [5], hole in the arm assembly [3], and hole in the bolt [7] to secure the bolt [5] and bolt [7]. (a) Use MS20995C32 lockwire, G01048 (preferred), or cable assembly, SPL-22466 (alternative), with a double twist method.

(c) Pull the spring scale, STD-759, slowly until the blade assembly [9] lifts off the windshield to measure the force. NOTE: One end of the wiper blade may remain on the windshield during this test. A piece of paper should be able to slide under the blade without binding. SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-006 (4) Adjust the force of the arm assembly [3]. NOTE: The wiper motor has a dimple at the end of the motor/converter shaft. (a) If the force is less than 6.5 lbf (28.9 N), tighten the adjustment screw [4] to increase the force of the arm assembly [3]. (b) If the force is more than 7.5 lbf (33.4 N), loosen the adjustment screw [4] to decrease the force of the arm assembly [3]. NOTE: The wiper motor is to be tensioned to the specified tension above, to be in compliance with AD 2003-20-13. SUBTASK 30-42-31-700-002 (5) If the force of the arm assembly [3] was adjusted per the steps above, measure the force of the arm assembly [3] again and adjust as required. SUBTASK 30-42-31-860-008 (6) Put the windshield wiper back to its usual position. (a) Turn the applicable wiper switch to the PARK position. 30-42-31 CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE WINDSHIELD WIPERS ON DRY GLASS. ALWAYS SUPPLY WATER TO THE WINDSHIELDS BEFORE YOU OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO A DRY WINDSHIELD. CAUTION DO NOT USE WATER WITH A HIGH MINERAL CONTENT . IF YOU DO NOT OBEY , IT CAN CAUSE MARKS ON THE COATED GLASS SURFACE. (b) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield. NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is recommended during the wiper test. 1) If water with a high mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth. (c) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name B 1 C00055 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WSHLD WIPER RIGHT B 3 C00054 ANTI-ICE & RAIN WSHLD WIPER LEFT (d) Make sure that the blade assembly [9] stops in the parked position. (e) Stop the water spray. F. Number Name B 7 C00395 WINDOW HEAT POWER R4 & R5 (INOP) RYR ALL SUBTASK 30-42-31-210-001 (3) Do these steps to examine the sweep pattern of the arm assembly [3]: CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE WINDSHIELD WIPERS ON DRY GLASS. ALWAYS SUPPLY WATER TO THE WINDSHIELDS BEFORE YOU OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO A DRY WINDSHIELD. (a) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield. NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth. (b) Turn the wiper switch to the LOW position and let the wiper operate for one or two cycles. (c) Turn the wiper switch to the PARK position. (d) Stop the water spray. (e) Make sure that the arm assembly [3] goes back to the parked position with the blade assembly [9] near the bottom edge of the windshield. (f) Make sure that the blade assembly [9] is parallel to the bottom edge of the windshield. 30-42-31 (g) Compare the sweep pattern to Figure 202. SUBTASK 30-42-31-820-005 (4) If you find a problem with the sweep pattern of the arm assembly [3], do these steps to adjust the sweep pattern: (a) Measure the distance that the tip of the blade assembly [9] needs to move. (b) Make a mark on the adjustment sleeve and wiper motor shaft to show the current installation. (c) Remove the windshield arm assembly [3] (TASK 30-42-31-000-801). (d) Re-install the windshield arm assembly [3] (TASK 30-42-31-400-801). NOTE: The arm assembly installation procedure has details on how to adjust the arm assembly. SUBTASK 30-42-31-860-012 (5) Put the wiper to its parked position. CAUTION DO NOT OPERATE THE WINDSHIELD WIPERS ON DRY GLASS. ALWAYS SUPPLY WATER TO THE WINDSHIELDS BEFORE YOU OPERATE THE WIPER. THE WIPER WILL CAUSE DAMAGE TO A DRY WINDSHIELD. (a) Supply a continuous spray of clean water to the windshield. NOTE: For windshields with a hydrophobic coating, deionized or distilled water is recommended during the wiper test. Water with a high mineral content can cause marks on the coated glass surface that are difficult to remove. If water with a high mineral content is used on a windshield with a hydrophobic coating, flush the windshield surface after the wiper test with deionized or distilled water, or remove the remaining water on the windshield with a cotton cloth. (b) Set the wiper switch to the LOW position. (c) Set the wiper switch to the PARK position. (d) Stop the water spray.

30-71-00-800-801Water and Drain Operation with 115 Volts on the Ground ATA 30 · Task 800-801

Task Description

2. Water and Drain Operation with 115 Volts on the Ground

A. General

(1) 28 volts AC is normally used to provide protection from icing for the water and drain system while the airplane is on the ground. 115 volt AC is used while the airplane is in the air. (2) This task should only be used when the 28 volt operation will not remove ice from the drain masts. (3) Refer to Cold Weather Maintenance Procedure, TASK 12-33-01-600-802.

B. References

Reference Title 12-33-01-600-802 Cold Weather Maintenance Procedure (P/B 301) 32-09-00-840-801 Prepare to Put the Airplane in the Air Mode (P/B 201) 32-09-00-860-801 Put the Airplane in the Air Mode (P/B 201) 32-09-00-860-802 Return the Airplane to the Ground Mode (P/B 201)

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 100 Lower Half of Fuselage 211 Flight Compartment - Left 212 Flight Compartment - Right

D. Procedure

SUBTASK 30-71-00-020-001 WARNING OBEY THE PROCEDURE THAT PREPARES TO PUT THE AIRPLANE IN THE AIR MODE. IN THE AIR MODE, MANY OF THE AIRPLANE SYSTEMS CAN OPERATE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURIES TO PERSONS AND DAMAGE TO EQUIPMENT CAN OCCUR. (1) Do this task: Prepare to Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-840-801. SUBTASK 30-71-00-020-002 (2) Do this task: Put the Airplane in the Air Mode, TASK 32-09-00-860-801. SUBTASK 30-71-00-020-003 (3) Make sure that this circuit breaker is closed: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name E 4 C00700 HEATERS DRAIN MAST AIR NOTE: This circuit breaker is opened in the Prepare for Air Mode Simulation task. It must be closed for the operation of the drain mast in the air (high heat) mode. 30-71-00

(b) Use (Figure 501) to identify access requirements. (c) Do the necessary tasks to access the heater and thermostat. 1) Do this task: Cargo Floor Panel - Removal, TASK 25-52-10-000-801. 2) Do this task: Forward Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Removal, TASK 25-52-17-000-801. 3) Do this task: Aft Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Removal, TASK 25-52-19-000-801. 30-71-00 4) Do this task: Cargo Compartment Sidewall Lining - Removal, TASK 25-52-06-000-801. 5) Do this task: Waste T ank Enclosure Panel - Removal, TASK 25-52-20-000-801. 6) Do this task: Carpet Removal, TASK 25-27-15-000-801. 7) Do this task: Entry and Service Area Floor Covering - Removal, TASK 25-27-21-000-801. SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-003 (2) If the heater is controlled by a thermostat apply the MS-242N spray, G02319, ice, G02320, or dry ice to decrease the temperature of the thermostat, (WDM 30-71-11). SUBTASK 30-71-00-720-004 (3) Make sure the heater gets warm. (a) You can use a Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572 or other temperature measuring device to make sure there is an increase of at least 10 degrees F (6 degrees C) above the ambient air temperature. (b) As an alternative, you can use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531 or equivalent to measure the current to the heater. (c) Current to the heater indicates that the heater is operating.

G. Put the Airplane Back to its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 30-71-00-410-002 (1) Replace or close the panels or doors used to access the heater and thermostat. (a) Do this task: Cargo Floor Panel - Installation, TASK 25-52-10-400-801. (b) Do this task: Forward Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Installation, TASK 25-52-17-400-801. (c) Do this task: Aft Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Installation, TASK 25-52-19-400-801. (d) Do this task: Cargo Compartment Sidewall Lining - Installation, TASK 25-52-06-400-801. (e) Do this task: Waste T ank Enclosure Panel - Installation, TASK 25-52-20-400-801. 30-71-06-000-801Heated Potable Water Hose Removal ATA 30 · Task 000-801

Task Description

2. Heated Potable Water Hose Removal (Figure 401)

A. General

(1) This task gives instructions to remove the Heated Potable Water Hose. (2) The procedures to remove the hoses are similar. Access to the hoses is either through the aft cargo compartment or through the floor near the aft lavatory.

B. References

Reference Title 20-10-52-000-801 Flexible Hose Removal (P/B 401) 25-27-15-000-801 Carpet Removal (P/B 401) 25-27-21-000-801 Entry and Service Area Floor Covering - Removal (P/B 401) 25-52-06-000-801 Cargo Compartment Sidewall Lining - Removal (P/B 401) 25-52-10-000-801 Cargo Floor Panel - Removal (P/B 401) 25-52-19-000-801 Aft Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Removal (P/B 401) 38-42-00-800-801 Potable Water System - Deactivation (P/B 201) SSM 30-71-11 System Schematics Manual WDM 30-71-11 Wiring Diagram Manual

C. Location Zones

Zone Area 141 Aft Cargo Compartment - Left 142 Aft Cargo Compartment - Right 240 Subzone - Passenger Compartment - Body Station 663.75 to Body Station 1016.00

D. Access Panels

Number Name/Location 822 Aft Cargo Door

E. Prepare for the Removal

SUBTASK 30-71-06-840-001 (1) Remove pressure from the potable water system, do this task: Potable Water System - Deactivation, TASK 38-42-00-800-801. SUBTASK 30-71-06-860-001 (2) Open these circuit breakers and install safety tags: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name D 18 C01463 WASTE/WTR LINE HEATERS E 5 C00233 HEATERS DRAIN 30-71-06 (Continued) CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name RYR 378-999 E 18 C01473 HOSE HEATERS RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-377 F 16 C01473 HOSE HEATERS RYR ALL SUBTASK 30-71-06-010-001 (3) Open this access panel: Number Name/Location 822 Aft Cargo Door SUBTASK 30-71-06-010-002 (4) Get access to the applicable heated potable water hose. (a) Determine the location of the heated water hoses (SSM 30-71-11). (b) Identify access requirements (Figure 401). (c) Do the applicable steps to get access the heated water hose: 1) Remove the cargo compartment floor panel, do this task: Cargo Floor Panel - Removal, TASK 25-52-10-000-801. 2) Remove the aft bulkhead liner, do this task: Aft Cargo Compartment Aft Bulkhead Liner - Removal, TASK 25-52-19-000-801. 3) Remove cargo compartment sidewall lining, do this task: Cargo Compartment Sidewall Lining - Removal, TASK 25-52-06-000-801. 4) Remove the carpet, do this task: Carpet Removal, TASK 25-27-15-000-801. 5) Remove the floor covering, do this task: Entry and Service Area Floor Covering - Removal, TASK 25-27-21-000-801. F. Heated Potable Water Hose Removal SUBTASK 30-71-06-020-001 (1) Disconnect the electrical connectors from the heated hose (WDM 30-71-11). SUBTASK 30-71-06-020-002 (2) Disconnect the fittings on the heated potable water hose. (a) Use a bucket to catch water which may be in the hose. (b) Remove the flexible hoses (TASK 20-10-52-000-801).

G. Heated Potable Water Hose Installation

SUBTASK 30-71-06-420-001 (1) Connect the heated potable water hose fittings. (a) For the fill fitting, tighten the heated hose to 620 ±80 in-lb (70 ±9 N·m). (b) For all other fittings, refer to the task: Flexible Hose Installation, TASK 20-10-52-400-801. SUBTASK 30-71-06-420-002 (2) Connect the electrical connectors to the heated water hose.

H. Heated Potable Water Hose Installation Test

SUBTASK 30-71-06-790-001 (1) Do a test for leaks in the potable water hose (TASK 38-10-00-790-801). SUBTASK 30-71-06-720-001 (2) Measure the temperature of the hose and surrounding area. SUBTASK 30-71-06-860-003 (3) Remove the safety tags and close these circuit breakers: CAPT Electrical System Panel, P18-3 Row Col Number Name D 18 C01463 WASTE/WTR LINE HEATERS E 5 C00233 HEATERS DRAIN RYR 378-999 E 18 C01473 HOSE HEATERS RYR 006, 007, 013, 014, 018-020, 043, 044, 056-063, 067-078, 081, 083-103, 111-114, 121-129, 131, 132, 135, 138-169, 174-179, 182-187, 189-203, 205-209, 212-220, 223, 224, 231-236, 239-298, 301-304, 306, 308, 315-317, 322-341, 343, 346-377 F 16 C01473 HOSE HEATERS RYR ALL SUBTASK 30-71-06-720-002 (4) Do this test of the heated hose: (a) If the temperature in the area is higher than 45 degrees F (7 degrees C), then decrease the temperature of the thermostat. NOTE: You can use MS-242N spray, G02319, ice, G02320, or compound, G01012 to decrease the temperature. Permit 3 to 5 minutes for the temperature of the thermostat to decrease before you check the temperature of the heated hose. The thermostat is in a bulge at the end of the hose. (b) Make sure the hose heater is powered by either of these methods: 1) Use a Infrared Thermometer, COM-1572, or equivalent, to make sure the hose gets warm. 30-71-06 2) Use a clamp-on current meter, COM-2531,or equivalent, to make sure that the heater is drawing electrical current.

I. Put the Airplane Back to Its Usual Condition

SUBTASK 30-71-06-670-001 (1) Disinfect the potable water system (TASK 38-10-00-600-801). SUBTASK 30-71-06-410-001 (2) Replace or close the panels or doors used to access the heated hose. WARNING SEAL THE CARGO COMPARTMENT WITH THE LINING. OBEY THE INSTRUCTIONS IN THE SPECIFIED PROCEDURE WHEN YOU INSTALL THE LINING. IF YOU INSTALL THE LINING INCORRECTLY , THE SMOKE CAN GO INTO THE PASSENGER COMPARTMENT DURING A FIRE. THIS CAN CAUSE INJURY TO PERSONNEL. (a) Re-install the cargo compartment sidewall lining (TASK 25-52-06-400-801). (b) Re-install the cargo compartment floor panel (TASK 25-52-10-400-801). (c) Re-install the aft bulkhead liner (TASK 25-52-19-400-801). (d) Re-install the floor covering (TASK 25-27-21-400-801). (e) Re-install the carpet (TASK 25-27-15-400-801). (f) Close this access door: Number Name/Location 822 Aft Cargo Door
